<<< IApplication.CreateCommand3 | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IApplication~CreateCommand3.md
P: ApiUuid: UUID of the add-in or non add-in application
P: CmdName: Unique name for the command
P: HelpID: ID of the context-sensitive help topic to open when F1 is pressed
P: ProductID: Product ID of the system help
P: Error: Status of creating the command as defined in dsCreateCommandError_e
R: Command
X: If CmdName already exists, then dsCreateCommand_e.dsCreateCommandError_CommandNameExisted is returned for Error.
Use the string specified for CmdName for IApplication::CreateUserCommand's UserCmdName parameter; e.g.; apiCmd.
To see a list of existing commands:
1. Right-click a toolbar in the user interface.
2. Select Customize Interface.
3. Click Commands on the left side of the dialog.
4. Use the scroll bar to see the list of existing commands.
5. To quickly locate a command that you've added:
1. Type the name of the command in Find.
2. Click the name of the command in the Name column.
3. Examine the information at the bottom of the dialog.
<<< IApplication.CreateCommand4 | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IApplication~CreateCommand4.md
P: ApiUuid: UUID of the add-in or non add-in application
P: GlobalCmdName: Unique name for the global command, which is not displayed to the user but which can be used as input
P: LocalCmdName: Unique name for the localized command, which is displayed to the user and which can be used as input
P: HelpID: ID of the context-sensitive help topic to open when F1 is pressed
P: ProductID: Product ID of the system help
P: Error: Status of creating the command as defined in dsCreateCommandError_e
R: Command
X: If either GlobalCmdName or LocalCmdName already exists, then dsCreateCommand_e.dsCreateCommandError_CommandNameExisted is returned for Error.
To see a list of existing commands:
1. Right-click a toolbar in the user interface.
2. Select Customize Interface.
3. Click Commands on the left side of the dialog.
4. Use the scroll bar to see the list of existing commands.
5. To quickly locate a command that you've added:
1. Type the name of the command in Find.
2. Click the name of the command in the Name column.
3. Examine the information at the bottom of the dialog.

P: Pattern: Pattern that defines the simple LineStyle (see Remarks)
R: True if the simple LineStyle is created, false if not
X: Simple LineStyle definition files are stored in ASCII text files with.lin filename extensions.
| These values... | Specify and create these simple LineStyle patterns... |
| Positive decimals | Length of dashes |
| Negative decimals | Length of spaces |
| 0 (zero) | Dots |
You must call ILineStyleManager::LoadLineStyle to load the simple LineStyle in a document before you can get the simple LineStyle.

<<< IApplication.CreateUserCommand | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IApplication~CreateUserCommand.md
P: ApiUuid: UUID of the add-in or non add-in application
P: UserCmdName: Command name for the menu item or the toolbar button's ToolTip (see Remarks)
P: CmdString: Command string (see Remarks)
P: Description: Description of the command
P: SmallIcon: Path image's file name for the command's small icon (see Remarks)
P: LargeIcon: Path and image's file name for the command's large icon (see Remarks)
P: UIState: Where to add the user command as defined in dsUIState_e
P: Error: Status of creating the command as defined in dsCreateCommandError_e
R: User command
X: If UserCmdName already exists, then dsCreateCommand_e.dsCreateCommandError_CommandNameExisted is returned for Error.
Specify the same string for UserCmdName as specified for IApplication::CreateCommand's CmdName parameter; e.g.; apiCmd.
The string to specify for CmdString is the same string specified for UserCmdName prefaced by ^C^C; e.g., ^C^CapiCmd.
To see a list of existing commands:
1. Right-click a toolbar in the user interface.
2. Select Customize Interface.
3. Click Commands on the left side of the dialog.
4. Use the scroll bar to see the list of existing commands.
5. To quickly locate a command that you've added:
1. Type the name of the command in Find.
2. Click the name of the command in the Name column.
3. Examine the information at the bottom of the dialog.
The recommended size for:
- SmallIcon is 16 x 16
- LargeIcon is 24 x 24.
The supported format for both images is.png (Portable Network Graphics bitmap image).

<<< IApplication.RunCommand2 | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IApplication~RunCommand2.md
P: CommandString: Name of command to run (see Remarks)
P: ParameterStrArray: Array of parameters to execute with the command
P: ParameterTypeLongArray: Array of ParameterStrArray types as defined by dsResType_e; the size of this array much match the size of ParameterStrArray
P: HideCommandTitle: True to hide the command in the command window, false to show the command in the command window
R: Status of running the command as defined in dsRunCommandError_e
X: Depending on the programming language that you use, either:
- append \n to the command
- or -
- include & Chr(10) after the command These control strings emulate a user pressing the Enter key in DraftSight. The number of control strings should correspond to the number of times a user is required to press the Enter key for the command in DraftSight.
For example:
| To delete all entities in a drawing in this programming language... | Call... |
| C# | int state; state = (int)dsApp. RunCommand (" DELETE ALL\n\n ", false); |
| VB.NET | Dim state as Integer; state = dsApp. RunCommand (" DELETE ALL" & Chr(10) & Chr(10), False) |
| VBA | Dim state as Long; state = dsApp. RunCommand (" DELETE ALL" & Chr(10) & Chr(10), False) |

<<< IArcAlignedText.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IArcAlignedText~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this curved text, call IArcAlignedText::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< IAttributeDefinition.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IAttributeDefinition~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry or IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this BlockAttribute definition, call IAttributeDefinition::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released or erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< IBlockDefinition.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IBlockDefinition~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this Block definition, call IBlockDefinition::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased before closing the drawing. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased extension dictionary, then that extension dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< ICommandMessage.PromptForBlockInsertion | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ICommandMessage~PromptForBlockInsertion.md
P: PreviewBlockName: Name of the Block definition to insert to create the Block instance
P: SpecifyScale: True to use the values specified for DefaultScaleX, DefaultScaleY, and DefaultScaleZ for the Block instance's scale factors, false to use the user-interactive values (see Remarks)
P: DefaultScaleX: Scale factor for the x axis for the Block instance if SpecifyScale is set to true (see Remarks)
P: DefaultScaleY: Scale factor for the y axis for the Block instance if SpecifyScale is set to true (see Remarks)
P: DefaultScaleZ: Scale factor for the z axis for the Block instance if SpecifyScale is set to true (see Remarks)
P: SpecifyRotation: True to use the value specified for DefaultRotation for the Block instance's rotation angle, false to use the user-interactive value (see Remarks)
P: DefaultRotation: Angle, in radians, by which to rotate the Block instance if SpecifyRotation is set to true (see Remarks)
P: X: x coordinate where to insert the Block instance
P: Y: y coordinate where to insert the Block instance
P: Z: z coordinate where to insert the Block instance
P: ScaleX: Scale factor for the x axis of the Block instance
P: ScaleY: Scale factor for the y axis of the Block instance
P: ScaleZ: Scale factor for the z axis of the Block instance
P: Rotation: Angle, in radians, by which to rotate the Block instance
R: Status of the input for inserting the Block instance as defined in dsPromptResultType_e
X: | If this parameter... | Is set to... | Then... |
| SpecifyScale | True | The DefaultScaleX, DefaultScaleY, and DefaultScaleZ values are used to scale the Block instance when the user presses the Enter key when prompted for these values in the command window. |
| | False | Either the values typed in the command window, or the coordinates that correspond to the point where the user clicks in the graphics area, are used to scale the Block instance. |
| SpecifyRotation | True | The DefaultRotation value is used for the rotation angle of the Block instance when the user presses the Enter key when prompted for this value in the command window. |
| | False | Either the value typed in the command window, or the angle that corresponds to the point where the user clicks in the graphics area, is used for the rotation angle of the Block instance. |
NOTE: A positive value for the rotation angle rotates the Block instance counterclockwise, and a negative value rotates the Block instance clockwise.
Typical use of this method involves:
1. Defining the Block definition using IDocument::CreateBlockDefinition, if needed. If a Block definition already exists, skip this step.
2. Defining how to insert the Block using ICommandMessage::PromptForBlockInsertion.
3. Inserting the Block using ISketchManager::InsertBlock2 and the output values of ICommandMessage::PromptForBlockInsertion.

Keywords are context-sensitive options that are available in the Options toolbar when executing a command. Keywords in LocalKeywordStrArray are displayed in the prompt in the command window during user interaction. Keywords in GlobalKeywordStrArray are not displayed in the prompt in the command window. However, users can type global keywords in the prompt in the command window if they know them.
If the PromptInit parameter is set to:
- dsPromptInit_e.dsPromptInit_NoDefault and a value is specified for DefaultValue, then the value is not used or displayed in the prompt in the command window.
- dsPromptInit_e.UsePromptExplanation, then call ICommandMessage::PromptExplanation to specify the text for PromptInit.

<<< ICustomData | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ICustomData.md
X: Custom data is non-graphical information that you can add to an entity in a drawing to supplement the data available via the entity's interface.
Custom data is made up of:
- Markers, which are stored as integers and which help identify the data type of the custom data. Valid values for markers are shown in the following table.
- Data, which are variable data types such as strings, doubles, integers, etc.
| Marker | Data | Data type |
| 1000 | Text | String |
| 1003 | Layer name | String |
| 1004 | Binary chunk | Variable-length binary array; maximum of 127 bytes |
| 1005 | Handle of an entity | String |
| 1010 − 1013 | Point | Three numbers |
| 1020 − 1059 | Double | Double |
| 1060 − 1070 | Integer | Signed 16-bit integer, also called Int16 |
| 1071 | Integer | Signed 32-bit integer, also called Int32 |
Custom data is stored in an entity using markers.
Because multiple applications can add custom data to an entity, use unique names to differentiate your custom data from that of other applications. Custom data names are stored in a drawing document's list of registered applications, which you can search to determine a unique name for your custom data.
After determining a unique name for your custom data, use an entity's:
- GetCustomData method to create the entity's CustomData object. Use the ICustomData methods to add data to the entity's CustomData object.
- SetCustomData method to change an entity's CustomData object.
- DeleteCustomData method to delete an entity's CustomData object.
- Handle property to get the entity's CustomData object's handle. Because entities can reference other entities by saving the handles of other entities in their custom data, handles do not have to be unique.
<<< ICustomData.Delete | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ICustomData~Delete.md
P: Index: Index of the custom data element
X: Call ICustomData::GetDataCount before calling this method to determine a valid value for Index.
<<< ICustomData.GetByteData | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ICustomData~GetByteData.md
P: Index: Index of the custom data element
R: Binary array (see Remarks)
X: Binary data for custom data is organized into variable-length chunks. The maximum length of each chunk is 127 bytes. If a binary array larger than 127 bytes was passed, then that data was not added. If an application requires more than 127 bytes of binary data, then the application must organize the data into multiple chunks.
Call ICustomData::GetDataCount before calling this method to determine a valid value for Index.

<<< IDictionary.Erased | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IDictionary~Erased.md
R: True to erase the dictionary and its entity association from the dictionary in which they exist, false to restore the dictionary to the dictionary in which it previously existed (see Remarks)
X: A dictionary does not have to be empty to be erased. Dictionary entries in the erased dictionary do not change.
| If this property is set to true... | Then the dictionary... |
| and the drawing remains open | can be restored by setting this property to false and calling IDictionary::SetEntry. NOTE: When a dictionary is erased, its entry and entity association are removed from the dictionary in which it existed. When an erased dictionary is restored, its entry and entity association in the dictionary in which it previously existed are not restored. You must call IDictionary::SetEntry to add the restored entry and entity association to the dictionary in which they previously existed. |
| when the drawing is closed | is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored. |
You can also use IDictionary::RemoveEntry to remove a dictionary from the dictionary in which it exists. You do not have to call IDictionary::SetEntry to restore a removed dictionary to the dictionary in which it previously existed if the removed dictionary was removed by IDictionary::RemoveEntry.

<<< IDictionary.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IDictionary~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the extension dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the extension dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must either remove or erase all XRecords from the extension dictionary before you can release and erase the extension dictionary from the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore the released and erased extension dictionary to this dictionary, call either IDictionary::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased before closing the drawing. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased extension dictionary, then that extension dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the extension dictionary does not exist.
<<< IDictionary.RemoveEntry | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IDictionary~RemoveEntry.md
P: Name: Name of the entity to remove from the dictionary
X: Only the entity that matches the specified name in this dictionary is removed. If the entity exists in the drawing, then the entity is not removed from the drawing.
NOTE: You can also remove dictionaries or XRecords in a dictionary using IDictionary::Erased or IXRecord::Erased, respectively. However, IXDictionary::Erased and IXRecord::Erased require you to call IDictionary::SetEntry to restore any dictionaries or XRecords erased using IDictionary::Erased or IXRecord::Erased. If you removed any dictionaries or XRecords using IDictionary::RemoveEntry, then you do not have to call IDictionary::SetEntry to restore the removed dictionaries or XRecords.

P: OldName: Name of the entity to change
P: NewName: Name to which to change OldName
R: True if OldName is changed to NewName, false if not

P: ApplicationName: Name of the custom data's registered application (see Remarks)
P: CustomData: Custom data
X: Call IDocument::GetRegisteredApplications to find out the names of the document's existing custom data.

P: Name: Name for Obj
P: Obj: Entity
X: | If the specified name... | Then... |
| Exists | The existing name and entity association is replaced with the name and entity association specified in this method. |
| Does not exist | The specified name and entity association is created. |

<<< IDimensionStyle.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IDimensionStyle~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this DimensionStyle, call IDimensionStyle::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< IDropData | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IDropData.md
X: This interface does not work in 2021 SP0.
A single QMimeData object can store the same data in several different formats (MIME types). This interface provides methods and properties that allow you to easily access drag and drop data for these specific MIME types:
- "text/plain"
- "text/html"
- "text/uri-list"
- "image/*"
- "application/x-color"
Other byte array data (e.g., "text/csv") and custom MIME types (e.g., "application/x-qt-windows-mime; value="<custom type>"") can be stored, retrieved, and removed using:
IDropData::GetData IDropData::SetData IDropData::RemoveFormat Implement _IDocumentEvents_DropNotifyEventHandler to notify you when a specific drop action has occurred. When the DropNotify event occurs, the delegate returns the drop data which you can explore using this interface.
This interface functions like Qt 5.15 > Qt Core > C++ Classes > QMimeData Class. See https://doc.qt.io/qt-5/qmimedata.html.

"text/plain" "text/html" "text/uri-list" "image/*", where * is the image format (e.g., "image/bmp") "application/x-color" Instead of calling this method with the common MimeTypes, use these getters:

R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this Ellipse or elliptical Arc, call IEllipseArc::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

To restore a released and erased dictionary for this external reference, call IExternalReference::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

| Passing these parameters... | Then the lines are reordered as... |
| (0, 3, 1) | (line2, line3, line4, line1, line5) |
| (0, 3, 2) | (line3, line4, line5, line1, line2) |

<<< ILayer.Frozen | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ILayer~Frozen.md
R: True if the Layer is frozen, false if the Layer is thawed
X: Freezing and thawing a layer controls the visibility and the ability to edit the Layer.

You cannot freeze the active layer.

<<< ILayer.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ILayer~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this Layer, call ILayer::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

R: Name of the PrintStyle
X: You can only set the name of a PrintStyle if named PrintStyles are used in the drawing. If color-dependent PrintStyles are used in the drawing, then the PrintStyle is set to ByColor and is read only. To determine the type of PrintStyle used in the document:
- call IDocument::GetCommandOptionString and specify dsCommandOptionString_e.dsCommandOptionString_GetNmPrnStyl for the Var parameter in a project, macro, or script.
- click Tools > Options > System Options, expand Printing, expand Default settings, and examine Default type in the user-interface.
<<< ILoft.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ILoft~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this Loft, call ILoft::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this model, call IModel::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)

To restore a released and erased dictionary for this DGN definition, call IPdfReference::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< IRevolve.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IRevolve~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this Revolve, call IRevolve::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< IRichLine.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IRichLine~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this RichLine, call IRichLine::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< ISketchManager.InsertEmbeddedObjectFromFile | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ISketchManager~InsertEmbeddedObjectFromFile.md
P: FilePath: Path and file name of the embedded object to insert
P: Link: True to insert a picture of the original file into the drawing, false to insert the contents of the file into the drawing (see Remarks)
P: DisplayAsIcon: True to display the application icon in the drawing, false to display it in the data
R: IEmbeddedObject
X: There are two ways to insert data from files:
- Linked object. Specify Link = true to insert a picture of the file into the drawing. When you edit a linked object, the source file changes. When you edit the source file, the linked object changes.
- Embedded object. Specify Link = false to insert the contents of the file into the drawing so that you may activate it using the source application that created it. Changes you make to the data in the embedded object do not appear in the original file, and changes you make to the original file do not appear in the drawing.

<<< ISketchManager.InsertHatchByBoundary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ISketchManager~InsertHatchByBoundary.md
P: BoundaryPointCountLongArray: Array containing a long that is the number of points in the Hatch boundary loop
P: BoundaryPointDblArray: Array of x-y coordinates of the points in the Hatch boundary loop
P: PatternName: Name of the Hatch pattern
P: PatternScale: Value by which to scale the Hatch pattern; values are between.25 and 2 in.25 unit increments; default is 1 (see Remarks)
P: PatternAngle: Angle of the Hatch pattern lines; values are between 0 and 2*pi radians in.26 radian increments; a positive angle rotates the pattern counter-clockwise
R: Hatch
X: If PatternScale is equal to:

- 0.25, then the distance between pattern lines is 1/4 the original distance.
PatternScale is valid only when the HatchPatternType parameter of IHatchPattern::GetHatchOrSolidData is not dsHatchPatternType_e.dsHatchPatternType_UserDefined.
To scale user-defined Hatch patterns, call IHatchPattern::SetHatchOrSolidData to change the line spacing of the Hatch pattern.

P: X1: x coordinate of the upper-left corner of the Note

P: Z1: z coordinate of the upper-left corner of the Note
P: X2: x coordinate of the lower-right corner of the Note
P: Y2: y coordinate of the lower-right corner of the Note
P: Z2: z coordinate of the lower-right corner of the Note
P: StrArray: Array of strings constituting the text of the Note
P: Angle: Angle of rotation of the Note
P: Height: Height of text in the Note
P: Justify: Justification as defined in dsNoteJustification_e

P: Width: Width of the Note
R: Note
X: DraftSight automatically calculates the height of the note's bounding box and modifies it based on the Justify parameter.
| This bounding box boundary... | Is calculated when Justify is... | |
| Top | dsNoteJustification_BottomLeft, dsNoteJustification_BottomCenter, or dsNoteJustification_BottomRight | |
| Bottom | dsNoteJustification_TopLeft, dsNoteJustification_TopCenter, or dsNoteJustification_TopRight | |
| Top and bottom | dsNoteJustification_MiddleLeft, dsNoteJustification_MiddleCenter, or dsNoteJustification_MiddleRight | |
The Width parameter setting determines word wrapping. The Height parameter is the default height of the note's text.

<<< ISketchManager.ScaleEntities | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ISketchManager~ScaleEntities.md
P: BasePointX: x coordinate of the base point (see Remarks)
P: BasePointY: y coordinate of the base point (see Remarks)
P: BasePointZ: z coordinate of the base point (see Remarks)
P: ScaleFactor: Factor by which to scale the entities (see Remarks)
P: EntityTypeLongArray: Array of longs of the types of entities to scale as defined dsObjectType_e
P: EntitiesArray: Array of objects of the entities to scale
X: The base point, if located on any of the specified entities, holds its original position during scaling, while all other points move by the same relative values in the direction of the x axis, y axis, and optional z axis. You can also select a point not on an existing entity as the base point. In this case, the distance from the base point to all other points making up the entity change by the same scale factor.
| A scaling factor... | Causes the entities to be... |
| larger than 1 | enlarged by this factor. |
| between 0 and 1 | reduced by this factor. |
For example, the scaling factor of 3 enlarges the specified entities three times, while a scaling factor of 0.25 reduces the entities to a quarter of their original size.

<<< ISolid.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ISolid~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this 2D Solid, call ISolid::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< ISolid3D.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ISolid3D~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this 3D solid, call ISolid3D::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< ITolerance.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ITolerance~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary exists and is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this Tolerance, call ITolerance::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.

<<< ITransparency | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.ITransparency.md
X: To change the transparency of an entity:
1. Get the entity's transparency object using either IEntityHelper::GetTransparency or the entity's Transparency property.
2. Determine if the entity's transparency object is by an alpha value, ByBlock, or ByLayer by calling one of the following methods:
- ITransparency::IsByAlpha
- ITransparency::IsByBlock
- ITransparency::IsByLayer
3. Call one of the following methods to change the transparency of that instance of the transparency object:
- ITransparency::SetByAlpha
- ITransparency::SetByBlock
- ITransparency::SetByLayer
4. Update the entity's transparency object using either IEntityHelper::SetTransparency or the entity's Transparency property.

<<< IUnknownEntity | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IUnknownEntity.md
X: An unknown entity:
- is an entity that is not officially supported by the DraftSight API.
- may be a custom entity.
- may be an unknown entity that has surfaced during the course of event handling.
During the course of application processing, unknown entities may surface that have no interface representation in the DraftSight API. If you capture unknown entities with the event handlers, _IDocumentEvents_ObjectAppendNotify, _IDocumentEvents_ObjectEraseNotify, or _IDocumentEvents_ObjectModifyNotify, then you can use this interface to recognize and process only ones with a specific IUnknownEntity::ClassName.
<<< IUnknownEntity.ClassName | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IUnknownEntity~ClassName.md

_IDocumentEvents_ObjectAppendNotify _IDocumentEvents_ObjectEraseNotify _IDocumentEvents_ObjectModifyNotify

<<< IUserCommand | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IUserCommand.md
X: To see a list of existing commands:
1. Right-click a toolbar in the user interface.
2. Select Customize Interface.
3. Click Commands on the left side of the dialog.
4. Use the scroll bar to see the list of existing commands.

1. Type the name of the command in Find.
2. Click the name of the command in the Name column.
3. Examine the information at the bottom of the dialog.

<<< IWorkspace | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IWorkspace.md
X: A workspace is a data container that combines user-interface elements such as menus, toolbars, and ribbon components. When a workspace is activated, all of its components appear.
As of DraftSight 2017 SP0, two types of workspaces are supported:
- Classic Default, which provides the traditional menu and toolbar s.
- Ribbon-based, which organizes groups of command s into tab s, panel s, row s, and row panel s. Adding a new ribbon-based workspace automatically creates a system menu and a quick access toolbar.

<<< IXRecord | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IXRecord.md
X: An XRecord's data is made up of:
- Markers, which are stored as integers and which help identify the data type of an XRecord's data. Valid values for markers are shown in the following table.
- Data, which are variable data types such as strings, doubles, integers, etc.
| Marker | Data | Data type |
| 1 | Text | String |
| 2 | Name | String |
| 3-4 | Text | String |
| 5 | Handle | String |
| 6-8 | Name | String |
| 9 | Text | String |
| 10-19 | Point | Three numbers |
| 20-49 | Double | Double |
| 50-59 | Angle | Double |
| 60-79 | Integer | Signed 16-bit integer, also called Int16 |
| 90-99 | Integer | Signed 32-bit integer, also called Int32 |
| 100-102 | Text | String |
| 105 | Handle of an entity | String |
| 110-119 | Point | Three numbers |
| 120-149 | Double | Double |
| 160-169 | Integer | Signed 64-bit integer, also called Int64 |
| 170-179 | Integer | Signed 16-bit integer, also called Int16 |
| 210-219 | Point | Three numbers |
| 220 | Double | Double |
| 221-223 | Point | Three numbers |
| 230 | Double | Double |
| 231-233 | Point | Three numbers |
| 270-279 | Integer | Signed 16-bit integer, also called Int16 |
| 280-289 | Integer | Signed 8-bit integer, also called Int8 |
| 290-299 | Boolean | Boolean |
| 300-309 | Text | String |
| 310-319 | Binary chunk | Variable-length binary array; maximum of 127 bytes |
| 320-369 | Handle of an entity | String |

<<< IXRecord.Erased | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IXRecord~Erased.md
R: True to erase the XRecord from the dictionary, false to restore it (see Remarks)
X: | If this property is set to true... | Then the XRecord... |
| and the drawing remains open | can be restored by setting this property to false and calling IDictionary::SetEntry. | when the drawing is closed | is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored. |

<<< IXRecord.ReleaseExtensionDictionary | draftsightapi/Interop.dsAutomation~Interop.dsAutomation.IXRecord~ReleaseExtensionDictionary.md
R: True if the dictionary is released and erased, false if not (see Remarks)
X: If the dictionary is not empty, i.e., it contains one or more XRecords, then it is not released and erased. You must remove or erase all XRecords from the dictionary before you can release and erase the dictionary. See IDictionary::RemoveEntry and IXRecord::Erased.
To restore a released and erased dictionary for this XRecord, call IXRecord::CreateExtensionDictionary or IDictionary::Erased. If you close the drawing before restoring the released and erased dictionary, then that dictionary is deleted from the drawing and cannot be restored.
This method also returns true if the dictionary does not exist.
